AP Calculus AB - Unit 1 Bank

Limits and Continuity | Canonical Bank | Build date 2026-08-19

This file is the master content factory for downstream Unit 1 resources. Teacher solutions are intentionally separable from student tasks.

Assessment Architecture

  • U1-MG01 Determine and interpret one-sided, two-sided, and overall limits from graphical and numerical information, distinguishing limiting behavior from the function value.
  • U1-MG02 Evaluate limits analytically by selecting and carrying out valid methods such as substitution, limit laws, factoring, conjugates, trigonometric limit reasoning, or squeeze reasoning when supported.
  • U1-MG03 Analyze continuity at points and on intervals, including piecewise boundaries, by coordinating limits, function values, and discontinuity classifications.
  • U1-MG04 Interpret infinite limits and end behavior and apply the Intermediate Value Theorem only when its hypotheses support the conclusion.

Progress language: Not Yet | Approaching | Meeting | Exceeding

Security: instructional items are separated from formative-secure Exit Tickets and secure Extended Reasoning / Performance / Summative items.

What's To Come5

1.1 What Is a Limit?

AP Topics: 1.1, 1.2

Learning target: Relate average versus instantaneous change to the idea of a limit and read and interpret limit notation.

What's To Come (1 items)

WTC01

U1-S1-WTC01U1-MG01DOK 2instructional
The blue curve is $y=x^2$ and the red line is a secant line. Imagine moving the second point of the secant closer and closer to the first point at $x=1$. Record what you expect to happen to the secant slope. Do not calculate a derivative.
Teacher answer / solution

Expected revisit: as the second point approaches x=1, the secant slope approaches 2, the tangent slope at x=1. Before instruction, a qualitative prediction is sufficient.

Notes Example (4 items)

EX01

U1-S1-NOTE-EX01U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

The values below come from $f(x)=x^2+1$ near $x=2$. Estimate the limit.

xf(x)
1.94.61
1.994.9601
2.015.0401
2.15.41
Teacher answer / solution

5

EX02

U1-S1-NOTE-EX02U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

A graph approaches the open point $(3,4)$ from both sides, but the filled point is $(3,9)$. State the limit and the function value.

Teacher answer / solution

limit 4; function value 9

EX03

U1-S1-NOTE-EX03U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

For $f(x)=x^2$, find the secant slope from $x=1$ to $x=1+h$ when $h=0.5$. Then predict what the slope approaches as $h\to0$.

Teacher answer / solution

secant 2.5; approaches 2

EX04

U1-S1-NOTE-EX04U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

Translate $\lim_{x\to -2}g(x)=6$ into a sentence about inputs and outputs.

Teacher answer / solution

As x approaches -2 from both sides, g(x) approaches 6.

Notes You Try It (4 items)

YTI01

U1-S1-NOTE-YTI01U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

Use nearby values $x=0.99,0.999,1.001,1.01$ for $q(x)=3x+2$ to estimate $\lim_{x\to1}q(x)$.

Teacher answer / solution

The outputs approach 5, so the limit is 5.

YTI02

U1-S1-NOTE-YTI02U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

Near $x=-1$, a curve approaches height 2 from both sides while $f(-1)=7$. State the limit and point value.

Teacher answer / solution

Limit = 2; $f(-1)=7$.

YTI03

U1-S1-NOTE-YTI03U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

For $f(x)=x^2$, find the secant slope from $x=2$ to $x=2.2$ and predict the limiting slope as the second x-value approaches 2.

Teacher answer / solution

Secant slope = 4.2; the slopes approach 4.

YTI04

U1-S1-NOTE-YTI04U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

Write in words what $\lim_{t\to4}P(t)=10$ communicates.

Teacher answer / solution

As t approaches 4 from both sides, P(t) approaches 10.

Practice Set 1 (18 items)

PS1-Q01

U1-S1-PS1-Q01U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM01
Which phrase best describes $\lim_{x\to 2} f(x)$?
  1. $f(2)$ must equal the limit.
  2. the value approached by $f(x)$ as $x$ gets arbitrarily close to 2
  3. the slope of the graph at every point
  4. the largest nearby function value
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. the value approached by $f(x)$ as $x$ gets arbitrarily close to 2

A limit describes nearby behavior and does not require the function value at the point.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 1.

PS1-Q02

U1-S1-PS1-Q02U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM02
Use the table to estimate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to 2} f(x)$.
xf(x)
1.9004.900
1.9904.990
1.9994.999
2.000undefined
2.0015.001
2.0105.010
2.1005.100
Teacher answer / solution

The values from both sides approach 5, so the estimated limit is 5.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 2.

PS1-Q03

U1-S1-PS1-Q03U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM03
Near $x=2$ the values of $f(x)$ approach 2, but $f(2)=7$. What is $\lim_{x\to 2}f(x)$?
  1. 7
  2. 0
  3. DNE
  4. 2
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: D. 2

The limit is determined by nearby values, not by the isolated function value.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 3.

PS1-Q04

U1-S1-PS1-Q04U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM04
For $f(x)=x^2$, compute the secant slope from $x=1$ to $x=1.5$.
Teacher answer / solution

$m=\dfrac{(1.5)^2-(1)^2}{0.5}=2.5$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 4.

PS1-Q05

U1-S1-PS1-Q05U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM05
For $a=1$, the table shows secant slopes. Estimate the instantaneous slope as $h\to0$.
hsecant slope for $x^2$ from $a$ to $a+h$
13
0.52.5
0.12.1
Teacher answer / solution

The secant slopes approach $2a=2$, so the instantaneous slope is approximately 2.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 5.

PS1-Q06

U1-S1-PS1-Q06U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM06
What does $$x\to 3^-$$ mean?
  1. the input equals the target only
  2. the output approaches from below
  3. x approaches 3 using values less than 3
  4. the function is decreasing
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. x approaches 3 using values less than 3

The superscript describes the side from which the input approaches.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 6.

PS1-Q07

U1-S1-PS1-Q07U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM07
Use the graph to estimate $\lim_{x\to 2}f(x)$ and state $f(2)$.
Teacher answer / solution

The curve approaches 4 from both sides, so the limit is 4. The filled point gives $f(2)=\lt strong\gt 7\lt /strong\gt $.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 7.

PS1-Q08

U1-S1-PS1-Q08U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM08
Classify the statement: If $f(2)$ is undefined then $\lim_{x\to2}f(x)$ must be DNE.
  1. False
  2. True
  3. Only for linear functions
  4. Cannot be determined
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. False

Limit reasoning concerns nearby behavior and both sides for a two-sided limit.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 8.

PS1-Q09

U1-S1-PS1-Q09U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM09
$s(t)$ gives distance traveled in miles as a function of time in hours. In words, what does $\lim_{h\to0}\dfrac{s(2+h)-s(2)}{h}$ represent?
Teacher answer / solution

It represents the instantaneous rate of change of the modeled quantity at the stated time, in output units per input unit.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 9.

PS1-Q10

U1-S1-PS1-Q10U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM10
The table leaves $f(0)$ undefined. Which conclusion is best supported?
xf(x)
-0.1002.900
-0.0102.990
-0.0012.999
0.000undefined
0.0013.001
0.0103.010
0.1003.100
  1. $f(0)=3$
  2. the limit does not exist
  3. $\lim_{x\to 0}f(x)=3$
  4. the function must be linear
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. $\lim_{x\to 0}f(x)=3$

Values on both sides approach the same number even though the center value is undefined.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 10.

PS1-Q11

U1-S1-PS1-Q11U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM11
Use the paired table to compare $\lim_{x\to 1}f(x)$ and $\lim_{x\to 1}g(x)$.
xf(x)g(x)
0.991.991.98
0.9991.9991.998
1.0012.0012.002
1.012.012.02
Teacher answer / solution

Both columns approach 2; therefore both limits are 2.

PS1-Q12

U1-S1-PS1-Q12U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM12
The inputs 1.9, 1.99, 1.999 approach 2. Which description is correct?
  1. they approach 2 from the left
  2. they equal 2 throughout
  3. they describe output values
  4. they prove the function is continuous
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. they approach 2 from the left

Compare each input with the target to identify the approach side.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 12.

PS1-Q13

U1-S1-PS1-Q13U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM13
A student says $\lim_{x\to 2}f(x)=7$ because $f(2)=7$. Nearby values instead approach 3. Identify the error and state the correct limit.
Teacher answer / solution

The student used the function value instead of nearby behavior. The correct limit is 3.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 13.

PS1-Q14

U1-S1-PS1-Q14U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM14
Write a complete limit statement showing that $f(x)$ approaches 6 as $x$ approaches 4.
Teacher answer / solution

$\displaystyle \lim_{x\to 4}f(x)=6$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 14.

Estimate the limit to three decimal places.
xq(x)
0.9901.41431
0.9991.41421
1.0011.41421
1.0101.41431
Teacher answer / solution

The outputs approach 1.414; estimated limit: 1.414.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 15.

PS1-Q16

U1-S1-PS1-Q16U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM16
Which expression is the secant slope of $f$ from $x=1$ to $x=1.2$?
  1. $\dfrac{f(1.2)-f(1)}{0.2}$
  2. $f(1.2)-f(1)$
  3. $\dfrac{f(1)}{0.2}$
  4. $\dfrac{f(1.2)}{1.2}$
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. $\dfrac{f(1.2)-f(1)}{0.2}$

Secant slope is change in output divided by change in input.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 16.

PS1-Q17

U1-S1-PS1-Q17U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM17
Which conclusion about changing only the single value $f(2)$ is most accurate?
  1. always forces the limit to be zero
  2. does not change $\lim_{x\to2}f(x)$ if nearby behavior is unchanged
  3. always makes the limit equal the function value
  4. has no mathematical effect anywhere
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. does not change $\lim_{x\to2}f(x)$ if nearby behavior is unchanged

Limits are controlled by values arbitrarily close to the target and, for two-sided limits, agreement from both sides.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 17.

PS1-Q18

U1-S1-PS1-Q18U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM18
The table suggests an approach value. Write the corresponding limit statement and explain what the notation means.
xf(x)
1.93.9
1.993.99
2.014.01
2.14.1
Teacher answer / solution

$\displaystyle \lim_{x\to 2}f(x)=4$. This means $f(x)$ can be made close to 4 by taking $x$ sufficiently close to 2 (with $x$ near, not necessarily equal to, 2).

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 18.

PS3-Q08

U1-S1-PS3-Q08U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM13
A student says $\lim_{x\to 3}f(x)=-2$ because $f(3)=-2$. Nearby values instead approach 5. Identify the error and state the correct limit.
Teacher answer / solution

The student used the function value instead of nearby behavior. The correct limit is 5.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 13.

PS3-Q09

U1-S1-PS3-Q09U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM09
$V(t)$ gives water volume in liters as a function of time in seconds. In words, what does $\lim_{h\to0}\dfrac{V(10+h)-V(10)}{h}$ represent?
Teacher answer / solution

It represents the instantaneous rate of change of the modeled quantity at the stated time, in output units per input unit.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 9.

PS3-Q10

U1-S1-PS3-Q10U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM17
Which conclusion about removing the point at $x=3$ while leaving the curve unchanged nearby is most accurate?
  1. does not by itself change the limit
  2. always forces the limit to be zero
  3. always makes the limit equal the function value
  4. has no mathematical effect anywhere
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. does not by itself change the limit

Limits are controlled by values arbitrarily close to the target and, for two-sided limits, agreement from both sides.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 17.

PS3-Q11

U1-S1-PS3-Q11U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM04
For $f(x)=x^2$, compute the secant slope from $x=-2$ to $x=-1.9$.
Teacher answer / solution

$m=\dfrac{(-1.9)^2-(-2)^2}{0.1}=-3.9$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 4.

PS3-Q12

U1-S1-PS3-Q12U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM12
The inputs 3.1, 3.01, 3.001 approach 3. Which description is correct?
  1. they equal 3 throughout
  2. they describe output values
  3. they approach 3 from the right
  4. they prove the function is continuous
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. they approach 3 from the right

Compare each input with the target to identify the approach side.

PS3-Q13

U1-S1-PS3-Q13U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM08
Classify the statement: To estimate a two-sided limit from a table only values to the right are needed.
  1. True
  2. Only for linear functions
  3. Cannot be determined
  4. False
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: D. False

Limit reasoning concerns nearby behavior and both sides for a two-sided limit.

PS3-Q14

U1-S1-PS3-Q14U1-MG01DOK 1instructionalU1-S1-PRACT-FAM16
Which expression is the secant slope of $f$ from $x=-1$ to $x=-0.9$?
  1. $\dfrac{f(-0.9)-f(-1)}{0.1}$
  2. $f(-0.9)-f(-1)$
  3. $\dfrac{f(-1)}{0.1}$
  4. $\dfrac{f(-0.9)}{-0.9}$
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. $\dfrac{f(-0.9)-f(-1)}{0.1}$

Secant slope is change in output divided by change in input.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 16.

1.2 Limits from Graphs and Tables

AP Topics: 1.3, 1.4, 1.9

Learning target: Determine one-sided and two-sided limits from graphs and tables, including when a limit does not exist.

What's To Come (1 items)

WTC01

U1-S2-WTC01U1-MG01DOK 2instructional
The graph has two different approach heights at $x=2$. Without using a formal rule yet, record what a traveler moving toward $x=2$ from the left would see and what a traveler moving from the right would see.
Teacher answer / solution

Expected revisit: left-hand limit 3 and right-hand limit 7; because they differ, the two-sided limit does not exist.

Notes Example (4 items)

EX01

U1-S2-NOTE-EX01U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

Use the table to find the left-hand and right-hand limits at x=2.

xf(x)
1.9002.900
1.9902.990
1.9992.999
2.0016.001
2.0106.010
2.1006.100
Teacher answer / solution

Left = 3; right = 6; two-sided limit DNE.

EX02

U1-S2-NOTE-EX02U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

At $x=-1$, both one-sided limits equal 5 but $f(-1)=2$. Determine the two-sided limit.

Teacher answer / solution

The two-sided limit is 5; the point value does not change the limit.

EX03

U1-S2-NOTE-EX03U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

A calculator table has outputs 4.99 and 4.999 to the left of x=3 and 5.001 and 5.01 to the right. State the three relevant limits.

Teacher answer / solution

Left = 5, right = 5, two-sided = 5.

EX04

U1-S2-NOTE-EX04U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

Explain why unequal one-sided limits force a two-sided limit to be DNE.

Teacher answer / solution

A two-sided limit requires a single common approach value from both directions.

A finite two-sided limit exists only when the left- and right-hand limits agree.

U1-S2-PS2-Q16U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S2-PRACT-FAM18
Given $\lim_{x\to -2^-}f(x)=-1$ and $\lim_{x\to -2^+}f(x)=4$, write a complete conclusion about the two-sided limit and justify it.
Teacher answer / solution

$\lim_{x\to -2^-}f(x)=-1$ and $\lim_{x\to -2^+}f(x)=4$. Since they differ, the two-sided limit is DNE.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 18.

PS2-Q17

U1-S2-PS2-Q17U1-MG01DOK 2instructionalU1-S2-PRACT-FAM08
Suppose both one-sided limits at $x=-1$ equal -2, but $f(-1)=2$. Which statement is true?
  1. the limit equals 2
  2. the limit is DNE
  3. $\lim_{x\to -1}f(x)=-2$
  4. the limit must be 0
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. $\lim_{x\to -1}f(x)=-2$

The point value may differ from an existing limit.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 8.

Write notation for the limit of $f(x)$ as $x$ approaches -3 using values greater than -3.
Teacher answer / solution

$\displaystyle \lim_{x\to -3^+}f(x)$.

U1-S2-CYU-Q02U1-MG01DOK 1instructional

Teacher answer / solution

Q03

U1-S2-CYU-Q03U1-MG01DOK 1instructional

What does $x\to4^-$ mean?

Teacher answer / solution

x approaches 4 using values less than 4.

Q04

U1-S2-CYU-Q04U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

A graph approaches height 6 from both sides at x=3 but has f(3)=0. Find the limit.

Teacher answer / solution

The limit is 6.

Q05

U1-S2-CYU-Q05U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

A table near x=1 has left outputs approaching -2 and right outputs approaching -2. What must be checked before claiming a two-sided limit?

Teacher answer / solution

That both sides approach the same value; here they do, so the limit is -2.

Q06

U1-S2-CYU-Q06U1-MG01DOK 2instructional

Explain in one sentence why averaging unequal one-sided limits is not a valid way to get a two-sided limit.

Teacher answer / solution

A two-sided limit requires agreement to one common value; averaging does not create that agreement.

1.3 Algebraic Limits

AP Topics: 1.5, 1.6, 1.7, 1.8

Learning target: Evaluate limits algebraically using direct substitution, limit laws, factoring, conjugates, and appropriate trigonometric or squeeze reasoning.

What's To Come (1 items)

WTC01

U1-S3-WTC01U1-MG02DOK 2instructional

Direct substitution into $\dfrac{x^2-9}{x-3}$ at $x=3$ gives $0/0$. Compare that result with the values of the expression at $x=2.9$, $2.99$, $3.01$, and $3.1$. What pattern is worth explaining?

Teacher answer / solution

Expected revisit: for x not equal to 3 the expression simplifies to x+3, so nearby values approach 6. The form 0/0 is indeterminate, not a conclusion.

Notes Example (4 items)

EX01

U1-S3-NOTE-EX01U1-MG02DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to2}(x^2+3x-1)$ by direct substitution.

Teacher answer / solution

$4+6-1=9$.

EX02

U1-S3-NOTE-EX02U1-MG02DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to4}\dfrac{x^2-16}{x-4}$ by factoring.

Teacher answer / solution

Factor to $(x-4)(x+4)/(x-4)$; limit = 8.

EX03

U1-S3-NOTE-EX03U1-MG02DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to9}\dfrac{\sqrt{x}-3}{x-9}$ using a conjugate.

Teacher answer / solution

After conjugate multiplication the expression is $1/(\sqrt{x}+3)$, so limit = $1/6$.

EX04

U1-S3-NOTE-EX04U1-MG02DOK 3instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to0}\dfrac{\sin(3x)}{x}$ and name the standard limit used.

Teacher answer / solution

$3\cdot\lim_{x\to0}\sin(3x)/(3x)=3$.

Notes You Try It (4 items)

YTI01

U1-S3-NOTE-YTI01U1-MG02DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to-1}(2x^2-x+4)$.

Teacher answer / solution

$2+1+4=7$.

YTI02

U1-S3-NOTE-YTI02U1-MG02DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to5}\dfrac{x^2-25}{x-5}$.

Teacher answer / solution

Cancel the common factor; limit = 10.

YTI03

U1-S3-NOTE-YTI03U1-MG02DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to16}\dfrac{\sqrt{x}-4}{x-16}$.

Teacher answer / solution

Conjugate gives $1/(\sqrt{x}+4)$; limit = $1/8$.

YTI04

U1-S3-NOTE-YTI04U1-MG02DOK 3instructional

Use the Squeeze Theorem to evaluate $\lim_{x\to0}x^2\sin(1/x)$.

Teacher answer / solution

$-|x|^2\le x^2\sin(1/x)\le |x|^2$ and both bounds approach 0, so the limit is 0.

PS1-Q03

U1-S3-PS1-Q03U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM03
Evaluate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to 3}\frac{x^2-9}{x-3}$.
Teacher answer / solution

Factor: $x^2-9=(x-3)(x+3)$. After cancellation the limit is $2(3)=\lt strong\gt 6\lt /strong\gt $.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 3.

PS1-Q04

U1-S3-PS1-Q04U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM04

Multiply by the conjugate. The expression becomes $1/(\sqrt{x}+2)$, so the limit is $\boxed{\frac{1}{4}}$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 4.

PS1-Q05

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 5.

PS1-Q06

U1-S3-PS1-Q06U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM06
Evaluate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to0}\frac{1-\cos(2x)}{x}$.
Teacher answer / solution

Since $1-\cos u$ is of order $u^2$, the quotient approaches 0. Equivalently use the conjugate and standard trig limits.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 6.

  4. direct substitution

Choose a method that resolves the observed form while preserving equivalent nearby behavior.

PS1-Q08

U1-S3-PS1-Q08U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM08
A student substitutes $x=2$ into $\frac{x^2-4}{x-2}$, gets $0/0$, and concludes the limit is DNE. Explain the error and evaluate the limit.
Teacher answer / solution

$0/0$ is an indeterminate form, not a conclusion. Factor and cancel $x-2$ for nearby $x$, leaving $x+2$; the limit is 4.

PS1-Q12

Use the Squeeze Theorem to evaluate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to0}x^2\cos(1/x)$ for even/positive power 2.
Teacher answer / solution

$-\lvert x\rvert^2\le x^2\cos(1/x)\le \lvert x\rvert^2$ and both bounds approach 0. Therefore the limit is 0.

PS1-Q13

Teacher answer / solution

For $x\ne 2$ the quotient equals $x+2$, whose values approach 4. A correctly constructed table samples that same nearby expression, so it approaches the same value.

U1-S3-PS2-Q10U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM03
Evaluate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to 2}\frac{x^2-4}{x-2}$.
Teacher answer / solution

Factor: $x^2-4=(x-2)(x+2)$. After cancellation the limit is $2(2)=\lt strong\gt 4\lt /strong\gt $.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 3.

PS2-Q11

U1-S3-PS2-Q11U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM17
Which strategy statement correctly matches an expression that simplifies after factoring?
  1. simplify for nearby x then take the limit
  2. the limit must be DNE
  3. always average left and right values
  4. replace the expression by its derivative
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. simplify for nearby x then take the limit

The strategy should respond to the algebraic structure of the limit.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 17.

Choose a method that resolves the observed form while preserving equivalent nearby behavior.

PS2-Q15

U1-S3-PS2-Q15U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM04
Evaluate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to 9}\frac{\sqrt{x}-3}{x-9}$.
Teacher answer / solution

Multiply by the conjugate. The expression becomes $1/(\sqrt{x}+3)$, so the limit is $\boxed{\frac{1}{6}}$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 4.

PS2-Q16

U1-S3-PS2-Q16U1-MG02DOK 3instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM18
Two students evaluate $\lim_{x\to 3}\frac{x^2-9}{x-3}$. One factors; the other expands a numerical table near 3. Explain why both should approach the same value and give that value.
Teacher answer / solution

For $x\ne 3$ the quotient equals $x+3$, whose values approach 6. A correctly constructed table samples that same nearby expression, so it approaches the same value.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 18.

PS2-Q17

U1-S3-PS2-Q17U1-MG02DOK 2instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM08
A student substitutes $x=3$ into $\frac{x^2-9}{x-3}$, gets $0/0$, and concludes the limit is DNE. Explain the error and evaluate the limit.
Teacher answer / solution

$0/0$ is an indeterminate form, not a conclusion. Factor and cancel $x-3$ for nearby $x$, leaving $x+3$; the limit is 6.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 8.

PS2-Q18

U1-S3-PS2-Q18U1-MG02DOK 3instructionalU1-S3-PRACT-FAM12
Use the Squeeze Theorem to evaluate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to0}x^3\cos(1/x)$ for even/positive power 3.
Teacher answer / solution

$-\lvert x\rvert^3\le x^3\cos(1/x)\le \lvert x\rvert^3$ and both bounds approach 0. Therefore the limit is 0.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 12.

1.4 Continuity and Discontinuity

AP Topics: 1.10, 1.11, 1.12, 1.13

Learning target: Analyze continuity at a point and on intervals, and classify discontinuities using limits, function values, and piecewise definitions.

What's To Come (1 items)

WTC01

U1-S4-WTC01U1-MG03DOK 2instructional
The curve approaches the same open point from both sides, but the function is assigned a different filled point at $x=2$. Decide what single edit to the function value would make the graph have no break at x=2.
Teacher answer / solution

Expected revisit: assign the function value to the common limit, 3, so the point fills the hole and the function becomes continuous at x=2.

Notes Example (4 items)

EX01

U1-S4-NOTE-EX01U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

Check the three continuity conditions at x=2 if $f(2)=5$ and $\lim_{x\to2}f(x)=5$.

Teacher answer / solution

The value exists, the limit exists, and they are equal; f is continuous at 2.

EX02

U1-S4-NOTE-EX02U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

Classify a discontinuity where the two-sided limit is 3 but the function value is 7.

Teacher answer / solution

Removable discontinuity.

EX03

U1-S4-NOTE-EX03U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

Find k so $f(x)=\begin{cases}kx+1,&x\lt 2\\7,&x\ge2\end{cases}$ is continuous.

Teacher answer / solution

Set $2k+1=7$, so $k=3$.

EX04

U1-S4-NOTE-EX04U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

State the intervals of continuity of $1/(x^2-4)$.

Teacher answer / solution

$(-\infty,-2)\cup(-2,2)\cup(2,\infty)$.

Notes You Try It (4 items)

YTI01

U1-S4-NOTE-YTI01U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

If $g(-1)=4$ and $\lim_{x\to-1}g(x)=2$, is g continuous at -1?

Teacher answer / solution

No. The limit and function value are unequal.

YTI02

U1-S4-NOTE-YTI02U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

Classify a discontinuity with left-hand limit -2 and right-hand limit 4.

Teacher answer / solution

Jump discontinuity.

YTI03

U1-S4-NOTE-YTI03U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

Find k so $g(x)=\begin{cases}kx-2,&x\lt 3\\10,&x\ge3\end{cases}$ is continuous.

Teacher answer / solution

Set $3k-2=10$, so $k=4$.

YTI04

U1-S4-NOTE-YTI04U1-MG03DOK 2instructional

State the intervals of continuity of $1/(x+3)$.

Teacher answer / solution

$(-\infty,-3)\cup(-3,\infty)$.

Practice Set 1 (18 items)

PS1-Q01

U1-S4-PS1-Q01U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM01
At $x=2$, $\lim_{x\to 2}f(x)=4$ and $f(2)=4$. Assume the two-sided limit exists. Is $f$ continuous at 2?
  1. not continuous
  2. continuous
  3. only right-continuous
  4. cannot be decided from any information
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. continuous

Continuity at a point requires the existing limit to equal the function value.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 1.

PS1-Q02

U1-S4-PS1-Q02U1-MG03DOK 1instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM02
Which continuity condition is described by: the two-sided limit exists?
  1. the derivative must exist
  2. the graph must cross the x-axis
  3. Condition 2: $\lim_{x\to a}f(x)$ exists
  4. the function must be linear
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. Condition 2: $\lim_{x\to a}f(x)$ exists

Continuity at a point coordinates definition, limit existence, and equality.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 2.

PS1-Q03

U1-S4-PS1-Q03U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM03
Classify the discontinuity when both sides approach 3 but $f(2)=7$.
  1. continuous
  2. oscillatory
  3. none
  4. removable
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: D. removable

A removable discontinuity has a finite common limit but a missing/wrong point; a jump has unequal finite one-sided limits.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 3.

PS1-Q04

U1-S4-PS1-Q04U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM04
Find $k$ so $f(x)=\begin{cases}kx+1,&x\lt 2\\7,&x\ge 2\end{cases}$ is continuous at $x=2$.
Teacher answer / solution

Set the one-sided expressions equal at the boundary: $2k+1=7$. Thus $k=\lt strong\gt 3\lt /strong\gt $.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 4.

Which statement about continuity at $x=2$ is correct?\lt ol class="choices" type="A"\gt \lt li\gt the function is discontinuous because the slope is nonzero\lt /li\gt \lt li\gt the limit is DNE\lt /li\gt \lt li\gt continuity cannot be discussed for linear functions\lt /li\gt \lt li\gt $f$ is continuous because the polynomial limit equals 7 = $f(2)$\lt /li\gt \lt /ol\gt \lt /div\gt \lt details class="teacher-answer"\gt \lt summary\gt Teacher answer / solution\lt /summary\gt \lt p\gt \lt strong\gt Answer:\lt /strong\gt D. $f$ is continuous because the polynomial limit equals 7 = $f(2)$\lt /p\gt \lt p\gt Linear functions are continuous everywhere.\lt /p\gt \lt p\gt \lt strong\gt Bank notes:\lt /strong\gt Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 11.\lt /p\gt \lt /details\gt \lt /article\gt \lt article class="bank-item" data-bank-id="U1-S4-PS1-Q12" data-destination="Practice Set 1" data-section="1.4" data-scope="section practice" data-mastery-goal-id="U1-MG03" data-evidence-point-id="" data-dok="2" data-item-type="short_response" data-security-level="instructional" data-figures=""\gt \lt h4\gt PS1-Q12\lt /h4\gt \lt div class="bank-meta"\gt \lt span\gt U1-S4-PS1-Q12\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt U1-MG03\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt DOK 2\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt instructional\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt U1-S4-PRACT-FAM12\lt /span\gt \lt /div\gt \lt div class="student-task"\gt For $f(x)=$1/(x-2)$$, identify the domain restriction(s) relevant to intervals of continuity.
Teacher answer / solution

Relevant restriction(s): 2. The function is continuous on each interval of its real domain.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 12.

PS1-Q13

U1-S4-PS1-Q13U1-MG03DOK 3instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM13
Critique the statement: $f(a)$ exists so $f$ is continuous at a.
Teacher answer / solution

The claim is incomplete; the two-sided limit must also exist and equal $f(a)$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 13.

PS1-Q14

U1-S4-PS1-Q14U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM14
For $f(x)=\begin{cases}kx+1,&x\lt 2\\7,&x\ge 2\end{cases}$, which value of $k$ makes $f$ continuous at 2?
  1. $6$
  2. $8$
  3. $3$
  4. $2$
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. $3$

Set the left-hand expression value at the boundary equal to the right-hand/function value.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 14.

PS1-Q15

U1-S4-PS1-Q15U1-MG03DOK 1instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM15
List the three conditions that must hold for $f$ to be continuous at $x=2$.
Teacher answer / solution

(1) $f(2)$ is defined. (2) $\lim_{x\to 2}f(x)$ exists. (3) $\lim_{x\to 2}f(x)=f(2)$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 15.

PS1-Q16

U1-S4-PS1-Q16U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM16
Which classification best fits: a finite common limit exists but the plotted point is at a different height?
  1. removable discontinuity
  2. always infinite discontinuity
  3. not enough to discuss limits
  4. a derivative test is required
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. removable discontinuity

Classify by the behavior of one-sided limits and the function value.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 16.

PS1-Q17

U1-S4-PS1-Q17U1-MG03DOK 3instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM17
A numerical estimate gives $\lim_{x\to 0}f(x)=3$. The graph marks $f(0)=8$. Reconcile the two representations and classify the function at 0.
Teacher answer / solution

The limit and point value are 3 and 8. Since they do not match, the function is discontinuous at 0.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 17.

PS1-Q18

U1-S4-PS1-Q18U1-MG03DOK 3instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM18
At $x=1$, suppose $\lim_{x\to 1^-}f(x)=2$, $\lim_{x\to 1^+}f(x)=2$, and $f(1)=2$. Decide continuity and identify the first continuity condition that fails, if any.
Teacher answer / solution

Continuous. The limit is 2 but the function value is 2, so the equality condition fails.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 18.

Practice Set 3 (18 items)

PS3-Q01

U1-S4-PS3-Q01U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM10
At a piecewise boundary $x=-1$, the left-hand limit is -2 and the right-hand limit is 3. What can you conclude about continuity?
Teacher answer / solution

The one-sided limits differ (-2 versus 3), so the function is discontinuous at -1 regardless of $f(-1)$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 10.

PS3-Q02

U1-S4-PS3-Q02U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM06
Use the graph to decide whether $f$ is continuous at $x=3$ and justify using the limit and function value.
Teacher answer / solution

The pieces meet at (3,-2) and the included point matches the common limit. Thus $\lim_{x\to 3}f(x)=f(3)=-2$ and the function is continuous.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 6.

PS3-Q03

U1-S4-PS3-Q03U1-MG03DOK 1instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM02
Which continuity condition is described by: the limit equals the function value?
  1. the derivative must exist
  2. Condition 3: $\lim_{x\to a}f(x)=f(a)$
  3. the graph must cross the x-axis
  4. the function must be linear
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. Condition 3: $\lim_{x\to a}f(x)=f(a)$

Continuity at a point coordinates definition, limit existence, and equality.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 2.

PS3-Q04

U1-S4-PS3-Q04U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM14
For $f(x)=\begin{cases}kx+4,&x\lt -1\\2,&x\ge -1\end{cases}$, which value of $k$ makes $f$ continuous at -1?
  1. $-2$
  2. $6$
  3. $2$
  4. $-1$
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. $2$

Set the left-hand expression value at the boundary equal to the right-hand/function value.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 14.

PS3-Q05

U1-S4-PS3-Q05U1-MG03DOK 3instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM18
At $x=-1$, suppose $\lim_{x\to -1^-}f(x)=-2$, $\lim_{x\to -1^+}f(x)=-2$, and $f(-1)=7$. Decide continuity and identify the first continuity condition that fails, if any.
Teacher answer / solution

Not continuous. The limit is -2 but the function value is 7, so the equality condition fails.

  1. continuous on that interval
  2. always infinite discontinuity
  3. not enough to discuss limits

PS3-Q15

U1-S4-PS3-Q15U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM03
Classify the discontinuity when both sides approach -2 but $f(4)$ is undefined.
  1. continuous
  2. removable
  3. oscillatory
  4. none
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. removable

A removable discontinuity has a finite common limit but a missing/wrong point; a jump has unequal finite one-sided limits.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 3.

PS3-Q16

U1-S4-PS3-Q16U1-MG03DOK 1instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM11
$f(x)=-1x+2$ for all real x. Which statement about continuity at $x=3$ is correct?
  1. the function is discontinuous because the slope is nonzero
  2. the limit is DNE
  3. $f$ is continuous because the polynomial limit equals -1 = $f(3)$
  4. continuity cannot be discussed for linear functions
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. $f$ is continuous because the polynomial limit equals -1 = $f(3)$

Linear functions are continuous everywhere.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 11.

PS3-Q17

U1-S4-PS3-Q17U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM07
Classify the discontinuity at $x=3$ from the graph and justify using one-sided limits.
Teacher answer / solution

The left-hand limit is -1 and the right-hand limit is 3. They differ, so the graph has a jump discontinuity.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 7.

PS3-Q18

U1-S4-PS3-Q18U1-MG03DOK 1instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM15
List the three conditions that must hold for $f$ to be continuous at $x=3$.
Teacher answer / solution

(1) $f(3)$ is defined. (2) $\lim_{x\to 3}f(x)$ exists. (3) $\lim_{x\to 3}f(x)=f(3)$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 15.

  2. oscillatory
  3. none
  4. jump
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: D. jump

A removable discontinuity has a finite common limit but a missing/wrong point; a jump has unequal finite one-sided limits.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 3.

Extra-Q05

U1-S4-EXTRA-Q05U1-MG03DOK 3instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM17
A numerical estimate gives $\lim_{x\to 4}f(x)=2$. The graph marks $f(4)=2$. Reconcile the two representations and classify the function at 4.
Teacher answer / solution

The limit and point value are 2 and 2. Since they match, the function is continuous at 4.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 17.

Teacher answer / solution

The limit must also equal the defined function value.

Extra-Q12

U1-S4-EXTRA-Q12U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM08
Given $\lim_{x\to 1}f(x)=5$ and $f(1)=0$, decide whether $f$ is continuous at 1 and justify.
Teacher answer / solution

No. The limit is 5 but $f(1)=0$, so equality fails.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 8.

Extra-Q13

U1-S4-EXTRA-Q13U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM04
Find $k$ so $f(x)=\begin{cases}kx+2,&x\lt 3\\9,&x\ge 3\end{cases}$ is continuous at $x=3$.
Teacher answer / solution

Set the one-sided expressions equal at the boundary: $3k+2=9$. Thus $k=\lt strong\gt \frac{7}{3}\lt /strong\gt $.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 4.

Extra-Q14

U1-S4-EXTRA-Q14U1-MG03DOK 3instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM18
At $x=3$, suppose $\lim_{x\to 3^-}f(x)=6$, $\lim_{x\to 3^+}f(x)=1$, and $f(3)=6$. Decide continuity and identify the first continuity condition that fails, if any.
Teacher answer / solution

Not continuous. The one-sided limits differ, so the two-sided limit does not exist.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 18.

Extra-Q15

Extra-Q16

The limit exists and equals 6; continuity still also requires $f(3)=6$.

Extra-Q17

U1-S4-EXTRA-Q17U1-MG03DOK 2instructionalU1-S4-PRACT-FAM06
Use the graph to decide whether $f$ is continuous at $x=1$ and justify using the limit and function value.
Teacher answer / solution

The pieces meet at (1,5) and the included point matches the common limit. Thus $\lim_{x\to 1}f(x)=f(1)=5$ and the function is continuous.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 6.

Continuity at a point coordinates definition, limit existence, and equality.

U1-S4-ETB-Q01

U1-S4-ETB-Q01U1-MG03DOK 2formative-secureU1-MG03-ETB-14-Q01

Find k so $f(x)=kx+1$ for x<2 and f(x)=7 for x>=2 is continuous.

Teacher answer / solution

2k+1=7 so k=3.

Expected evidence: Solves a continuity parameter condition.

Scoring / rubric: 2-point evidence: 1 point correct mathematical conclusion; 1 point supporting work/justification when requested.

Representation: piecewise symbolic

Calculator / reference rule: No calculator unless stated

U1-S4-ETB-Q02

U1-S4-ETB-Q02U1-MG03DOK 2formative-secureU1-MG03-ETB-14-Q02

Explain why the equality equation is the correct condition.

Teacher answer / solution

Continuity requires the left-hand boundary limit to equal the function/right-hand value.

Expected evidence: Connects the parameter equation to continuity.

Scoring / rubric: 2-point evidence: 1 point correct mathematical conclusion; 1 point supporting work/justification when requested.

Representation: justification

Calculator / reference rule: No calculator unless stated

Exit Ticket C (2 items)

U1-S4-ETC-Q01

U1-S4-ETC-Q01U1-MG03DOK 2formative-secureU1-MG03-ETC-14-Q01

The left-hand limit at x=-1 is 4 and the right-hand limit is -2. Classify the discontinuity and identify the failed condition.

Teacher answer / solution

Jump discontinuity; the two-sided limit does not exist.

Expected evidence: Classifies and links to a continuity condition.

Scoring / rubric: 2-point evidence: 1 point correct mathematical conclusion; 1 point supporting work/justification when requested.

Representation: one-sided limits

U1-S4-ETC-Q02

U1-S4-ETC-Q02U1-MG03DOK 2formative-secureU1-MG03-ETC-14-Q02

State intervals of continuity for $1/(x-3)$.

Teacher answer / solution

$(-\infty,3)\cup(3,\infty)$.

Expected evidence: Identifies continuity on the real domain.

Scoring / rubric: 2-point evidence: 1 point correct mathematical conclusion; 1 point supporting work/justification when requested.

Representation: symbolic/domain

1.5 Asymptotes and IVT

AP Topics: 1.14, 1.15, 1.16

Learning target: Interpret infinite limits and limits at infinity to identify asymptotic behavior, and apply the Intermediate Value Theorem when its hypotheses are satisfied.

What's To Come (1 items)

WTC01

U1-S5-WTC01U1-MG04DOK 2instructional
The graph becomes unbounded near one vertical line and flattens toward one horizontal line far from the origin. Identify those two lines from the picture and describe what the function is doing near each.
Teacher answer / solution

Expected revisit: vertical asymptote x=2 with infinite one-sided behavior; horizontal asymptote y=0 because the function approaches 0 as |x| grows.

Notes Example (4 items)

EX01

U1-S5-NOTE-EX01U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

For $f(x)=1/(x-3)$, state the vertical asymptote and one-sided infinite limits.

Teacher answer / solution

VA x=3; left limit $-\infty$ and right limit $+\infty$.

EX02

U1-S5-NOTE-EX02U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to\infty}(4+3/x)$ and state the horizontal asymptote.

Teacher answer / solution

Limit = 4; HA y=4.

EX03

U1-S5-NOTE-EX03U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to\infty}(3x^2+1)/(2x^2-5)$.

Teacher answer / solution

Equal degrees give ratio 3/2.

EX04

U1-S5-NOTE-EX04U1-MG04DOK 3instructional

$f$ is continuous on [0,4], with $f(0)=2$ and $f(4)=9$. Does IVT guarantee a c with $f(c)=6$? Explain.

Teacher answer / solution

Yes. 6 lies between 2 and 9, and continuity on the closed interval is given.

Notes You Try It (4 items)

YTI01

U1-S5-NOTE-YTI01U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

For $g(x)=2/(x+1)$, state the vertical asymptote and one-sided behavior.

Teacher answer / solution

VA x=-1; left $-\infty$, right $+\infty$.

YTI02

U1-S5-NOTE-YTI02U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to\infty}(-2+5/x^2)$.

Teacher answer / solution

Limit = -2; HA y=-2.

YTI03

U1-S5-NOTE-YTI03U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to-\infty}(5x^3-1)/(4x^3+2)$.

Teacher answer / solution

Equal degrees give ratio 5/4.

YTI04

U1-S5-NOTE-YTI04U1-MG04DOK 3instructional

$g$ is continuous on [1,5], with $g(1)=8$ and $g(5)=3$. Does IVT guarantee a c with $g(c)=10$?

Teacher answer / solution

No. 10 is not between the endpoint values 8 and 3, so these data do not trigger the IVT guarantee.

Practice Set 1 (18 items)

PS1-Q01

U1-S5-PS1-Q01U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM01
If $\lim_{x\to 2}f(x)=\infty$, which conclusion is supported?
  1. horizontal asymptote at y=0
  2. vertical asymptote at x=2
  3. the function is continuous at the target
  4. the two-sided finite limit equals 0
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. vertical asymptote at x=2

Unbounded behavior near a finite x-value indicates a vertical asymptote.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 1.

PS1-Q02

U1-S5-PS1-Q02U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM02
Find $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to\infty}3+2/x$ and state the horizontal asymptote.
Teacher answer / solution

The reciprocal term approaches 0, so the limit is 3 and the horizontal asymptote is $y=3$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 2.

PS1-Q03

U1-S5-PS1-Q03U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM03
Use the graph to identify the vertical and horizontal asymptotes. Then describe the one-sided behavior near the vertical asymptote.
Teacher answer / solution

Vertical asymptote: $x=2$. Horizontal asymptote: $y=0$. For $1/(x-2)$, the left side tends to $-\infty$ and the right side tends to $\infty$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 3.

PS1-Q04

U1-S5-PS1-Q04U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM04
Evaluate $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{2x^2+1}{1x^2-3}$.
Teacher answer / solution

Equal degrees: use the ratio of leading coefficients. Limit = $2$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 4.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 9.

PS1-Q10

U1-S5-PS1-Q10U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM10
For $f(x)=1/(x-2)$, describe the one-sided behavior at $x=2$ and state the vertical asymptote.
Teacher answer / solution

As $x\to 2^-$, $f(x)\to-\infty$; as $x\to 2^+$, $f(x)\to\infty$. The vertical asymptote is $x=2$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 10.

PS1-Q11

U1-S5-PS1-Q11U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM11
For end behavior, what conclusion matches: numerator degree less than denominator degree?
  1. always x=0
  2. always DNE
  3. the function must be continuous everywhere
  4. $y=0$
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: D. $y=0$

Compare dominant terms as x grows in magnitude.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 11.

PS1-Q12

U1-S5-PS1-Q12U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM12
A model has the long-term behavior: concentration approaches 2 mg/L over a long time. Write the corresponding limit as $t\to\infty$ and interpret it.
Teacher answer / solution

$\displaystyle \lim_{t\to\infty}C(t)=2$. The model approaches 2 in the stated output units for large time.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 12.

PS1-Q13

U1-S5-PS1-Q13U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM13
$f$ is continuous on [0,4] with endpoint values 2 and 8. For target 5, does IVT guarantee a solution?
  1. No
  2. Yes
  3. Only if f is linear
  4. Only if the midpoint works
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. Yes

Check continuity and whether the target lies between endpoint values.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 13.

PS1-Q14

U1-S5-PS1-Q14U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM14
For an attempted IVT argument, f has a jump inside the interval. What is the best assessment?
  1. IVT always applies to any function
  2. the exact solution is the midpoint
  3. the continuity hypothesis fails
  4. a vertical asymptote is guaranteed
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. the continuity hypothesis fails

An IVT argument must explicitly verify continuity and the between-values condition.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 14.

PS1-Q15

U1-S5-PS1-Q15U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM15
Find the end behavior limit $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to-\infty}\frac{1x^3+2}{3x^3-1}$.
Teacher answer / solution

Equal degrees imply the ratio of leading coefficients: $\frac{1}{3}$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 15.

PS1-Q16

U1-S5-PS1-Q16U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM16
Which statement is sufficient to conclude that $x=2$ is a vertical asymptote of $f$?
  1. at least one one-sided limit as x approaches 2 is infinite
  2. $f(2)=0$
  3. $\lim_{x\to\infty}f(x)=2$
  4. f is continuous at the target
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. at least one one-sided limit as x approaches 2 is infinite

A vertical asymptote is signaled by unbounded one-sided behavior near a finite input.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 16.

PS1-Q17

U1-S5-PS1-Q17U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM17
A student writes: "Because 6 is between $f(0)=1$ and $f(4)=9$, IVT guarantees $f(c)=6$." What crucial hypothesis is missing?
Teacher answer / solution

The student must also establish that $f$ is continuous on [0,4]. Without continuity, IVT cannot be invoked.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS1; coverage family 17.

Teacher answer / solution

$\displaystyle \lim_{x\to\infty}f(x)=-2$, so the horizontal asymptote is $y=-2$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 18.

PS2-Q17

U1-S5-PS2-Q17U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM08
Classify the IVT statement: IVT requires continuity on the closed interval under discussion.
  1. False
  2. Only for polynomials
  3. True
  4. Only with a calculator
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. True

IVT is an existence theorem under continuity and between-value hypotheses; it does not locate the input.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 8.

PS2-Q18

U1-S5-PS2-Q18U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM12
A model has the long-term behavior: temperature approaches 68 F as time grows. Write the corresponding limit as $t\to\infty$ and interpret it.
Teacher answer / solution

$\displaystyle \lim_{t\to\infty}T(t)=68$. The model approaches 68 in the stated output units for large time.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS2; coverage family 12.

Practice Set 3 (18 items)

PS3-Q01

U1-S5-PS3-Q01U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM10
For $f(x)=1/(x-3)$, describe the one-sided behavior at $x=3$ and state the vertical asymptote.
Teacher answer / solution

As $x\to 3^-$, $f(x)\to-\infty$; as $x\to 3^+$, $f(x)\to\infty$. The vertical asymptote is $x=3$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 10.

PS3-Q02

U1-S5-PS3-Q02U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM06
$f$ is continuous on [-1,0], with $f(-1)=2$ and $f(0)=5$. Which statement is guaranteed by IVT?
  1. there is some c in (-1,0) with $f(c)=3$
  2. $f(-0.5)=3$
  3. f has a horizontal asymptote
  4. f is increasing on the interval
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. there is some c in (-1,0) with $f(c)=3$

The target lies between the endpoint values and continuity supplies existence, not location or monotonicity.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 6.

PS3-Q03

U1-S5-PS3-Q03U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM02
Find $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to\infty}4-7/x$ and state the horizontal asymptote.
Teacher answer / solution

The reciprocal term approaches 0, so the limit is 4 and the horizontal asymptote is $y=4$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 2.

PS3-Q04

U1-S5-PS3-Q04U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM14
For an attempted IVT argument, the function is continuous and the target is between endpoint outputs. What is the best assessment?
  1. IVT always applies to any function
  2. the exact solution is the midpoint
  3. IVT applies
  4. a vertical asymptote is guaranteed
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. IVT applies

An IVT argument must explicitly verify continuity and the between-values condition.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 14.

PS3-Q07

U1-S5-PS3-Q07U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM01
If $\lim_{x\to 3}f(x)=\infty$, which conclusion is supported?
  1. horizontal asymptote at y=0
  2. vertical asymptote at x=3
  3. the function is continuous at the target
  4. the two-sided finite limit equals 0
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: B. vertical asymptote at x=3

Unbounded behavior near a finite x-value indicates a vertical asymptote.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form PS3; coverage family 1.

PS3-Q08

U1-S5-PS3-Q08U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM13
$f$ is continuous on [-2,2] with endpoint values 7 and 1. For target 4, does IVT guarantee a solution?
  1. No
  2. Only if f is linear
  3. Yes
  4. Only if the midpoint works
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. Yes

Check continuity and whether the target lies between endpoint values.

Limit = \lt strong\gt $1$\lt /strong\gt .\lt /p\gt \lt p\gt \lt strong\gt Bank notes:\lt /strong\gt Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 4.\lt /p\gt \lt /details\gt \lt /article\gt \lt article class="bank-item" data-bank-id="U1-S5-EXTRA-Q14" data-destination="Extra Practice" data-section="1.5" data-scope="section practice" data-mastery-goal-id="U1-MG04" data-evidence-point-id="" data-dok="2" data-item-type="short_response" data-security-level="instructional" data-figures=""\gt \lt h4\gt Extra-Q14\lt /h4\gt \lt div class="bank-meta"\gt \lt span\gt U1-S5-EXTRA-Q14\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt U1-MG04\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt DOK 2\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt instructional\lt /span\gt \lt span\gt U1-S5-PRACT-FAM18\lt /span\gt \lt /div\gt \lt div class="student-task"\gt For $f(x)=$x^4/(2x^4+1)$$, determine the horizontal asymptote using a limit at infinity and state the limit notation.
Teacher answer / solution

$\displaystyle \lim_{x\to\infty}f(x)=\frac{1}{2}$, so the horizontal asymptote is $y=\frac{1}{2}$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 18.

Extra-Q15

U1-S5-EXTRA-Q15U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM14
For an attempted IVT argument, only a right-hand limit at one endpoint is known but continuity on the interval is not established. What is the best assessment?
  1. IVT always applies to any function
  2. the exact solution is the midpoint
  3. the continuity hypothesis is not established
  4. a vertical asymptote is guaranteed
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: C. the continuity hypothesis is not established

An IVT argument must explicitly verify continuity and the between-values condition.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 14.

Extra-Q16

U1-S5-EXTRA-Q16U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM10
For $f(x)=1/(x-1)$, describe the one-sided behavior at $x=1$ and state the vertical asymptote.
Teacher answer / solution

As $x\to 1^-$, $f(x)\to-\infty$; as $x\to 1^+$, $f(x)\to\infty$. The vertical asymptote is $x=1$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 10.

Extra-Q17

U1-S5-EXTRA-Q17U1-MG04DOK 3instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM06
$f$ is continuous on [1,2], with $f(1)=8$ and $f(2)=10$. Which statement is guaranteed by IVT?
  1. there is some c in (1,2) with $f(c)=9$
  2. $f(1.5)=9$
  3. f has a horizontal asymptote
  4. f is increasing on the interval
Teacher answer / solution

Answer: A. there is some c in (1,2) with $f(c)=9$

The target lies between the endpoint values and continuity supplies existence, not location or monotonicity.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 6.

Extra-Q18

U1-S5-EXTRA-Q18U1-MG04DOK 2instructionalU1-S5-PRACT-FAM02
Find $\displaystyle \lim_{x\to\infty}2+1/x^2$ and state the horizontal asymptote.
Teacher answer / solution

The reciprocal term approaches 0, so the limit is 2 and the horizontal asymptote is $y=2$.

Bank notes: Parallel practice form Extra; coverage family 2.

CYU (6 items)

Q01

U1-S5-CYU-Q01U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

If $\lim_{x\to2^+}f(x)=\infty$, what asymptotic feature is indicated?

Teacher answer / solution

x=2 is a vertical asymptote.

Q02

U1-S5-CYU-Q02U1-MG04DOK 1instructional

Teacher answer / solution

5.

Q03

U1-S5-CYU-Q03U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

Find the horizontal asymptote of $(3x^2+1)/(x^2-4)$.

Teacher answer / solution

y=3.

Q04

U1-S5-CYU-Q04U1-MG04DOK 2instructional

State the two hypotheses you must verify before using IVT on [a,b] to guarantee f(c)=N.

Teacher answer / solution

f is continuous on [a,b], and N lies between f(a) and f(b).

Q05

U1-S5-CYU-Q05U1-MG04DOK 3instructional

If f is continuous on [0,3], f(0)=-2, and f(3)=5, does IVT guarantee a zero?

Teacher answer / solution

Yes. 0 lies between -2 and 5.

Q06

U1-S5-CYU-Q06U1-MG04DOK 3instructional

Why does IVT not identify the exact value of c?

Teacher answer / solution

It is an existence theorem; the hypotheses guarantee at least one input but do not locate it.

Unit-Level Inventories

Unit Review (20 items)

Q01

U1-REV-Q01U1-MG01DOK 1instructional

Write $\lim_{x\to2}f(x)=5$ in words.

Teacher answer / solution

As x approaches 2 from both sides, f(x) approaches 5.

U1-EXT01U1-MG01DOK 3secureU1-EXT-E01

Teacher answer / solution

The claim is not supported. Both sides trend toward 5 as x gets closer to 2, so the evidence supports a limit of 5. The farther samples should not outweigh the convergence pattern nearer the target.

Expected evidence: Reconciles asymmetric finite samples and defends a two-sided limit estimate.

Teacher answer / solution

The slope is $((2+h)^2-4)/h=4+h$, giving 5, 4.5, 4.1, 4.01. These values approach 4, so the instantaneous slope is 4.

U1-EXT04

U1-EXT04U1-MG02DOK 3secureU1-EXT-E04

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to4}(x^2-16)/(\sqrt{x}-2)$ by selecting an efficient sequence of algebraic transformations. Show why each transformation is valid for nearby x.

Teacher answer / solution

Factor $x^2-16=(x-4)(x+4)$ and use $x-4=(\sqrt{x}-2)(\sqrt{x}+2)$. Cancel the common factor for nearby x not equal to 4. The remaining expression is $(x+4)(\sqrt{x}+2)$, which approaches $8\cdot4=32$.

Expected evidence: Selects and chains equivalent algebraic transformations to resolve an indeterminate form.

Scoring / rubric: 4-point analytic reasoning rubric: mathematical correctness 2; justification/communication 2.

Representation: symbolic/strategy

Calculator / reference rule: No calculator unless stated

Bank notes: Intended secure use: reserve/tool-only or summative_candidate; do not expose instructionally.

U1-EXT05

U1-EXT05U1-MG02DOK 3secureU1-EXT-E05

Two methods are proposed for $\lim_{x\to0}(1-\cos x)/x^2$: a conjugate argument and a numerical table. Explain which can provide an analytical justification and complete that method.

Teacher answer / solution

Use the conjugate: $(1-\cos x)/x^2=(1-\cos^2x)/(x^2(1+\cos x))=(\sin x/x)^2/(1+\cos x)$. The limit is $1/(1+1)=1/2$. A table can support an estimate but is not the analytical justification.

Expected evidence: Distinguishes numerical evidence from an analytical limit derivation and completes the latter.

Scoring / rubric: 4-point analytic reasoning rubric: mathematical correctness 2; justification/communication 2.

Representation: symbolic/method comparison

Calculator / reference rule: No calculator unless stated

Bank notes: Intended secure use: reserve/tool-only or summative_candidate; do not expose instructionally.

At x=1 the one-sided limits already agree, so assigning f(1) to the common limit repairs continuity. At x=4 the one-sided limits differ; changing the single value f(4) cannot make those nearby approach values agree.

Performance Task (2 items)

U1-PERF01

U1-PERF01U1-MG03|U1-MG04DOK 3secureU1-PERF01-E03

Continuity Repair Brief. A controller uses $$F(x)=\begin{cases}kx+1,&x\lt 2\m,&x=2\\7-x,&x\gt 2.\end{cases}$$ (a) Determine the right-hand limit at x=2. (b) Find k so the two one-sided limits agree. (c) Find m so F is continuous at x=2. (d) If instead m=9 while k keeps the value from part (b), classify the discontinuity. (e) With the continuous version of F, use the Intermediate Value Theorem to decide whether F(c)=4 is guaranteed for some c in [1,4]. State the hypotheses you use.

Teacher answer / solution

(a) Right side: 7-2=5. (b) Set 2k+1=5, giving k=2. (c) Continuity requires m=5. (d) The common limit is 5 but the point value is 9, so the discontinuity is removable. (e) The repaired function is continuous on [1,4]. To make an IVT guarantee, use a subinterval whose endpoint outputs bracket 4: F(1)=3 and F(2)=5. Since 4 lies between 3 and 5 and F is continuous on [1,2], IVT guarantees at least one c in (1,2) with F(c)=4. (The explicit formula gives c=1.5 on that branch; a second solution occurs at x=3.)

Expected evidence: Coordinates limits and values to repair a piecewise function and invokes IVT only when its conditions support the claim.

Scoring / rubric: 8-point task rubric: (a)-(c) 1 point each; (d) 2 points; (e) 3 points for theorem conditions and conclusion.

Representation: piecewise symbolic/theorem reasoning

Calculator / reference rule: No calculator required

Bank notes: Evidence points: U1-PERF01-E03 for MG03 and U1-PERF01-E04 for MG04. Complete student product is a worked continuity repair and theorem justification.

U1-PERF02

U1-PERF02U1-MG02|U1-MG04DOK 3secureU1-PERF02-E04

Cooling-Model Analysis. A sensor model for t>=0 is $$C(t)=2+\frac{10}{t+2}.$$ (a) Find C(0) and C(8). (b) Determine $\lim_{t\to\infty}C(t)$ and interpret the result in context as the long-run sensor reading. (c) Without first solving C(t)=4, use the Intermediate Value Theorem to determine whether at least one time in [0,8] must have C(t)=4. (d) Now solve C(t)=4 exactly and compare the exact result with the theorem conclusion. (e) Explain why the vertical-asymptote issue at t=-2 does not invalidate the IVT argument on [0,8].

Teacher answer / solution

(a) C(0)=7 and C(8)=3. (b) The reciprocal term approaches 0, so the long-run limit is 2; the reading approaches 2 units. (c) C is continuous on [0,8] because t+2 is never zero there, and 4 lies between 7 and 3. IVT guarantees at least one c with C(c)=4. (d) $2+10/(t+2)=4$ gives $10/(t+2)=2$, so t+2=5 and t=3. The exact solution confirms the existence result. (e) The denominator zero at t=-2 lies outside [0,8], so C is continuous throughout the interval used for IVT.

Expected evidence: Interprets end behavior and applies IVT with explicit domain/continuity reasoning; then verifies by analytical solution.

Scoring / rubric: 10-point task rubric: endpoint evaluation 2; end behavior 2; IVT argument 3; exact solve 2; interval-domain explanation 1.

Representation: contextual symbolic/theorem reasoning

Calculator / reference rule: No calculator unless stated

Bank notes: Evidence points: U1-PERF02-E02 for analytical manipulation and U1-PERF02-E04 for asymptote/IVT reasoning. Complete product is a worked model analysis.

Teacher answer / solution

Expected evidence: States continuity and between-value hypotheses and gives a precise existence conclusion.

Continuity on the closed interval is given and 6 lies between 9 and 2. Therefore IVT guarantees at least one $c\in(2,5)$ with $f(c)=6$. The theorem does not identify or guarantee uniqueness of c.

Expected evidence: States continuity and between-value hypotheses and gives a precise existence conclusion.
